30	## 2. Topology and trust boundaries
31	
32	```
33	 browser  (apps/web on Modal Server)  ── anonymous id in localStorage + cookie mirror
34	    │  HTTPS JSON + SSE (reconnecting, Last-Event-ID); every request: X-Faultline-User: u_<uuid>
35	    ▼
36	 faultline-harness
37	    ├─ api          FastAPI @modal.asgi_app. No provider secret. Validates the user id, owns the HTTP
38	    │               contract (§5), spawns run_episode, and reads/writes state ONLY through Store.
39	    ├─ Store        @app.cls(max_containers=1, min_containers=1, volumes={"/data": faultline-db}).
40	    │               The single SQLite writer (§4). No secret, no fault plan, no network beyond Modal.
41	    └─ run_episode  @app.function(secrets=[anthropic-secret]). The ONLY code that sees ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
42	                    Model loop over MCP tools; appends events to Store; evaluates; always deletes the episode.
43	    │  HTTPS: gym REST (reset / observe / evaluate / delete) + MCP streamable HTTP at /mcp (step)
44	    ▼
45	 faultline-sandbox-env
46	    └─ api          FastAPI + FastMCP. Stateless containers; episode state {sandbox_id, fault_plan,
47	                    ledger, baseline} in modal.Dict "faultline-episodes". Every tool call: load episode →
48	                    consult fault plan → (maybe) exec in the Sandbox → append to the ledger.
49	    │  modal.Sandbox.from_id(sid).exec(...)   block_network=True, no secrets, one per episode
50	    ▼
51	 Modal Sandbox      fixture repo at /workspace, python3 + pytest. Sees nothing else.
52	```
53	
54	What each process can see:
55	
56	| Process | Provider key | Fault plan / grader | Workspace files | Persistent DB | Network |
57	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
58	| browser | ✗ | ✗ (events about faults, after the fact) | via observe/events | via api only | harness only |
59	| harness `api` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | via Store RPC | Store + spawn |
60	| harness `Store` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ (only what events carry) | ✓ (sole writer) | Volume only |
61	| harness `run_episode` | ✓ | ✗ | via MCP only | via Store RPC | Anthropic + sandbox-env |
62	| sandbox-env `api` | ✗ | ✓ | via Sandbox API | ✗ | Modal Sandbox only |
63	| Modal Sandbox (agent's shell) | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ | ✗ | none |
64	
65	Why three apps: the harness must be replaceable (different model / prompt) without touching the
66	environment; the environment must be gradeable without trusting the agent; the UI is static. The secret
67	boundary is enforced by Modal's per-function secrets: `api` asserts at startup that `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
68	is *absent* from its environment and reports `has_provider_key: false` on `/health`.
69	
70	## 3. Life of a run
71	
72	1. **Identity.** On first load the SPA mints `u_<uuid4>`, stores it in `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]`
73	   and mirrors it to the `faultline_uid` cookie. Every API call carries `X-Faultline-User`. `GET /me`
74	   upserts the `users` row.
75	2. **Conversation.** The user picks a scenario in the composer. `POST /conversations {scenario_id}` creates
76	   a `conversations` row titled after the scenario. A conversation is a thread of runs; the user's
77	   "message" is the task prompt (default = scenario prompt, editable before Run).
78	3. **Run.** `POST /conversations/{id}/runs {model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` → Store creates a
79	   `runs` row (`queued`) → `api` calls `run_episode.spawn(run_id, …)` and returns `{run_id}` immediately
80	   (Modal web requests are capped at 150 s, so nothing long-running happens inside a request).
81	4. **Episode.** `run_episode` calls `POST /episodes` on sandbox-env (reset: create Sandbox, upload
82	   fixture, apply sticky faults, snapshot baseline), emits `run.started` and `episode.reset`, then loops:
83	   `messages.create` → for each `tool_use` call the MCP tool with `X-Faultline-Episode` → emit
84	   `tool.call` / `tool.result` (+ `fault.fired` from the ledger delta, `workspace.diff` from observe after
85	   mutating calls) → repeat until `submit`, `end_turn`, `max_steps` or an unrecoverable API error.
86	   Every `messages.create` also emits `llm.call` (usage, stop reason, request id, attempt).
87	5. **Persistence.** Events are appended to Store in per-step batches
88	   (`Store().append_events.remote(run_id, events)`), which inserts `events` rows and, in the same
89	   transaction, projects them into `messages` / `blocks` / `llm_calls` (§4.5). The loop keeps the events in
90	   memory too and re-sends the full list at `run.finished` (idempotent), so a Store restart self-heals.
91	6. **Streaming.** The browser tails `GET /runs/{id}/events` (SSE). `api` polls `Store.events_after(run_id,
92	   after_seq)` every 400 ms, closes the stream at ~110 s, and the browser reconnects with
93	   `Last-Event-ID` (= `events.seq`). A page refresh restores from `GET /runs/{id}` (whole record) or
94	   `GET /conversations/{id}` (persisted transcript) and resumes the tail.
95	7. **Grading.** `POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate` uploads hidden tests, runs pytest, removes them, and scores
96	   recovery checks from the ledger (`score = 60·tests_pass + 40·weighted checks`). `episode.evaluated`
97	   and `run.finished` update `runs.score / evaluation_json / status / tokens`; `DELETE /episodes/{id}`
98	   runs in `finally`.
99	8. **Checkpoint.** Store snapshots the DB to the Volume after `run.finished` (§4.3).
100	
101	## 4. Persistence layer: SQLite on a Modal Volume
102	
103	### 4.1 Requirements
104	
105	Persist, per anonymous browser user: conversations, runs, the full event log of each run, and the LLM
106	turns including tool calls and their results, in a shape that can (a) render a chat transcript without
107	replaying events, and (b) rebuild the exact `messages=[…]` array the model saw at each step. Survive page
108	reloads, browser restarts, and redeploys. No external database service.
109	
110	### 4.2 Modal facts that constrain the design
111	
112	- Volumes are "write-once, read-many". Writes are visible to other containers only after a commit
113	  (Modal background-commits every few seconds and on container shutdown; `.commit()` forces one).
114	  Other containers must `.reload()` to see committed changes, and reload fails while files are open.
115	- "Last write wins in case of concurrent modification of the same file" — there is no distributed file
116	  locking. Concurrent writers to one SQLite file would corrupt it.
117	- The mount is a FUSE filesystem. SQLite's WAL mode relies on a shared-memory-mapped `-shm` file and
118	  POSIX advisory locks; their behaviour on the mount is undocumented. A background commit is not a
119	  point-in-time snapshot across files, so a live DB + its journal could be captured inconsistently.
120	- Modal's own SQLite example (the Datasette cron) builds the database on local disk, copies it onto the
121	  Volume, and commits. That is the pattern to copy.
122	- Volume v2 must be requested explicitly (`version=2`); the 1.5.5 CLI still marks it experimental. v1 is
123	  fine for one file.
124	
125	### 4.3 Design: one writer, hot copy on local disk, consistent snapshots on the Volume
126	
127	```
128	 api ──.remote()──▶ ┌───────────────── Store (1 container) ─────────────────┐
129	 run_episode ──────▶│ /tmp/faultline.sqlite3   WAL, synchronous=NORMAL, FK on │
130	                    │   ▲ restore on @modal.enter        checkpoint ▼         │
131	                    │ /data/faultline.sqlite3  ◀── VACUUM INTO .tmp + os.replace + vol.commit()
132	                    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
133	                                   Modal Volume "faultline-db" (v1)
134	```
135	
136	- **Single writer.** `Store` is a Modal class with `max_containers=1`. It is the only process that ever
137	  opens the database, so SQLite's locking assumptions hold trivially and nobody needs `reload()` — the
138	  reader *is* the writer. `min_containers=1` keeps it warm (an SSE poll must not pay a cold start plus a
139	  restore). `@modal.concurrent(max_inputs=32)` lets many `api` polls and one run's appends interleave; a
140	  `threading.Lock` serialises access to the single `sqlite3` connection (ops are sub-millisecond).
141	- **Hot copy on local disk.** The live file is `/tmp/faultline.sqlite3` on the container's own disk with
142	  ordinary SQLite settings (`journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`,
143	  `busy_timeout=5000`). No FUSE questions.
144	- **Checkpoint.** `VACUUM INTO '/data/faultline.sqlite3.tmp'` writes a complete, consistent, single-file
145	  copy; `os.replace()` renames it over `/data/faultline.sqlite3`; `vol.commit()` publishes it. Because
146	  only complete files are ever renamed into place, a background commit can never capture a half-written
147	  database. Triggers: after every `run.finished`, every 15 s while there are unsaved writes, and in
148	  `@modal.exit`. A `checkpoint_now` local entrypoint forces one for evidence/export.
149	- **Restore.** `@modal.enter` copies `/data/faultline.sqlite3` → `/tmp` if present (else creates an empty
150	  DB), then applies migrations: `PRAGMA user_version` gates `harness/migrations/000N_*.sql`, applied in
151	  order inside one transaction each.
152	- **Durability model.** Completed runs are durable as soon as their post-`run.finished` checkpoint commits
153	  (seconds). If the Store container dies mid-run, at most the last ≤15 s of that run's events are lost
154	  from the Volume — and they are not lost from the system: `append_events` is idempotent on
155	  `(run_id, seq)` (`INSERT OR IGNORE`), and `run_episode` re-sends its complete in-memory event list at
156	  `run.finished`, which back-fills any gap. A redeploy briefly overlaps old and new Store containers; the
157	  same idempotent re-send covers the overlap for runs that finish, and the ≤15 s window is documented for
158	  runs that don't.
159	- **Ids.** `<prefix>_<hex ms timestamp><12 hex random>` (sortable, no extra dependency): `c_`, `r_`, `m_`,
160	  `b_`, `l_`. User ids are minted by the browser as `u_<uuid4>`.
161	- **Migration from the current code.** `harness/store.py` today is `RunStore` over the Modal Dict
162	  `faultline-runs` (one whole `RunRecord` per run, re-put on every event, 200-entry index). The
163	  `RunStore` interface (`get / create / list_runs`) is kept as a thin client over `Store` so `loop.py`
164	  and `api.py` change minimally; the loop switches from whole-record puts to `append_events` per step
165	  plus a full idempotent re-send at `run.finished`; the Dict is dropped.
166	- **Ops.** `modal volume get faultline-db faultline.sqlite3 runs/` yields a consistent file for local
167	  `sqlite3` inspection and for the evidence folder. Retention: none for the take-home (a `DELETE`
168	  archives; nothing is hard-deleted).
169	- **Known limits (accepted).** One Store container; full-file snapshot per checkpoint. Comfortable to
170	  hundreds of MB; past that, ship incrementally (Litestream-style, or `modal-mosql`) or move to Postgres.
171	  A single-writer actor is also a throughput ceiling (thousands of events/s — orders of magnitude above
172	  need).
173	
174	### 4.4 Schema (`services/agent-harness/harness/migrations/0001_init.sql`)
175	
176	```sql
177	-- Faultline harness store. All timestamps ISO-8601 UTC ("…Z"). JSON columns hold pydantic dumps
178	-- from faultline_common.schemas. This file is canonical once it lands; until then, this doc is.
179	PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON;
180	
181	CREATE TABLE users (
182	  id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                    -- u_<uuid4>, minted by the browser (§6)
183	  created_at    TEXT NOT NULL,
184	  last_seen_at  TEXT NOT NULL,
185	  user_agent    TEXT
186	);
187	
188	CREATE TABLE conversations (
189	  id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                    -- c_…
190	  user_id       TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
191	  scenario_id   TEXT NOT NULL,
192	  title         TEXT NOT NULL,                       -- defaults to the scenario title; PATCHable
193	  created_at    TEXT NOT NULL,
194	  updated_at    TEXT NOT NULL,                       -- bumped on every run event (drives list order)
195	  archived_at   TEXT                                 -- soft delete
196	);
197	CREATE INDEX ix_conversations_user ON conversations(user_id, updated_at DESC);
198	
199	CREATE TABLE runs (
200	  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                  -- r_…
201	  conversation_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES conversations(id),
202	  user_id         TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),-- denormalised for ownership checks
203	  scenario_id     TEXT NOT NULL,
204	  model           TEXT NOT NULL,
205	  seed            INTEGER,
206	  max_steps       INTEGER NOT NULL,
207	  task_prompt     TEXT NOT NULL,                     -- what the user turn actually contained
208	  episode_id      TEXT,                              -- sandbox-env episode (set after reset)
209	  status          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('queued','running','ok','error','truncated')),
210	  score           REAL,                              -- 0..100 from evaluate
211	  evaluation_json TEXT,                              -- EvaluateResponse (checks, tests, ledger)
212	  input_tokens    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
213	  output_tokens   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
214	  error           TEXT,
215	  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
216	  started_at      TEXT,
217	  finished_at     TEXT
218	);
219	CREATE INDEX ix_runs_conversation ON runs(conversation_id, created_at);
220	CREATE INDEX ix_runs_live ON runs(status) WHERE status IN ('queued','running');
221	
222	-- Append-only event log: the source of truth for replay, SSE resume and evidence export.
223	CREATE TABLE events (
224	  run_id  TEXT    NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
225	  seq     INTEGER NOT NULL,                          -- 0-based; == Event.id == SSE id / Last-Event-ID
226	  ts      TEXT    NOT NULL,
227	  type    TEXT    NOT NULL,                          -- EventType (run.started … log, llm.call, turn.thinking)
228	  step    INTEGER,
229	  data    TEXT    NOT NULL,                          -- JSON: Event.data
230	  PRIMARY KEY (run_id, seq)
231	) WITHOUT ROWID;
232	
233	-- Transcript projection in Anthropic message shape: what the UI renders and what the model saw.
234	CREATE TABLE messages (
235	  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                  -- m_…
236	  conversation_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES conversations(id),
237	  run_id          TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
238	  seq             INTEGER NOT NULL,                  -- order within the conversation
239	  role            TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('user','assistant')),
240	  step            INTEGER,                           -- harness step that produced it (NULL for the task prompt)
241	  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
242	  UNIQUE (conversation_id, seq)
243	);
244	
245	CREATE TABLE blocks (
246	  id           TEXT PRIMARY KEY,                     -- b_…
247	  message_id   TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES messages(id),
248	  seq          INTEGER NOT NULL,                     -- order within the message
249	  type         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (type IN ('text','thinking','tool_use','tool_result')),
250	  text         TEXT,                                 -- text / thinking / tool_result output (already capped upstream)
251	  tool_name    TEXT,                                 -- tool_use
252	  tool_use_id  TEXT,                                 -- tool_use and tool_result (join key)
253	  input_json   TEXT,                                 -- tool_use input
254	  is_error     INTEGER,                              -- tool_result
255	  exit_code    INTEGER,                              -- tool_result (run_command)
256	  duration_ms  INTEGER,                              -- tool_result
257	  fault_json   TEXT,                                 -- FaultFired echoed on the result, if any
258	  truncated    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
259	  UNIQUE (message_id, seq)
260	);
261	CREATE INDEX ix_blocks_tool_use ON blocks(tool_use_id);
262	
263	-- One row per messages.create: the "LLM turn". Retries on 429/5xx get attempt > 1.
264	CREATE TABLE llm_calls (
265	  id                 TEXT PRIMARY KEY,               -- l_…
266	  run_id             TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES runs(id),
267	  step               INTEGER NOT NULL,
268	  attempt            INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
269	  model              TEXT NOT NULL,
270	  request_id         TEXT,                           -- anthropic `request-id` response header
271	  stop_reason        TEXT,                           -- end_turn | tool_use | max_tokens | …
272	  input_tokens       INTEGER,
273	  output_tokens      INTEGER,
274	  cache_read_tokens  INTEGER,
275	  cache_write_tokens INTEGER,
276	  started_at         TEXT NOT NULL,
277	  duration_ms        INTEGER,
278	  error              TEXT,
279	  UNIQUE (run_id, step, attempt)
280	);
281	
282	PRAGMA user_version = 1;
283	```
284	
285	Relationships: `users 1─∞ conversations 1─∞ runs 1─∞ events`; `runs 1─∞ messages 1─∞ blocks`;
286	`runs 1─∞ llm_calls`. `events` is the source of truth; `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` are projections
287	written in the same transaction (dropping them is scope cut #5 in the plan — the web reducer can rebuild
288	a transcript from events alone).
289	
290	### 4.5 Event → transcript projection
291	
292	Performed inside `Store.append_events`, per event, in the transaction that inserts the event row:
293	
294	| Event | Effect |
295	|---|---|
296	| `run.started` | `runs.status='running'`, `started_at`; `conversations.updated_at` |
297	| `episode.reset` | `runs.episode_id`; user message (`step NULL`) with one `text` block = `task_prompt` |
298	| `turn.thinking`, `turn.text`, `tool.call` (same `step`) | one assistant message for that step (created on first block); blocks appended in event order: `thinking`, `text`, `tool_use{tool_name, tool_use_id, input_json}` |
299	| `tool.result` (same `step`) | one user message for that step (Anthropic requires tool results in a user turn); `tool_result{tool_use_id, text=output, is_error, exit_code, duration_ms, fault_json}` |
300	| `llm.call` | `llm_calls` row; running totals into `runs.input_tokens/output_tokens` |
301	| `episode.evaluated` | `runs.score`, `runs.evaluation_json`; assistant message with the submit summary |
302	| `run.finished` | `runs.status`, `finished_at`, `error`, final usage; `conversations.updated_at`; marks the DB dirty for checkpoint |
303	| `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`, `log` | event row only; the UI reads them from the event log |
304	
305	Rebuilding the model's view of step *n*: take the conversation's messages for the run with `seq` up to
306	the assistant message of step *n − 1* plus its tool-result message, expand blocks in `seq` order, map
307	`text/thinking/tool_use/tool_result` one-to-one onto Anthropic content blocks. The UI instead groups each
308	`tool_result` under its `tool_use` by `tool_use_id` and renders it as a tool chip inside the assistant turn.
309	
310	## 5. HTTP contracts
311	
312	### 5.1 Harness API (`faultline-harness/api`, CORS `*`, all routes below `/scenarios` and `/health` require `X-Faultline-User`)
313	
314	| Route | Body → Response |
315	|---|---|
316	| `GET /health` | `Health{svc:"harness", ok, model_default, sandbox_env_url, has_provider_key:false, detail:{store:{ok,last_checkpoint_at}}}` |
317	| `GET /scenarios` | proxied from sandbox-env (public fields only) |
318	| `GET /me` | upsert → `{user_id, conversations: n}` |
319	| `GET /conversations` | `[{id, title, scenario_id, created_at, updated_at, last_run:{id,status,score}|null}]` newest first, archived excluded |
320	| `POST /conversations` | `{scenario_id, title?}` → `Conversation` |
321	| `GET /conversations/{id}` | `{conversation, runs:[RunSummary], messages:[Message{blocks:[Block]}]}` |
322	| `PATCH /conversations/{id}` | `{title?, archived?}` → `Conversation` |
323	| `DELETE /conversations/{id}` | soft archive → `{archived:true}` |
324	| `POST /conversations/{id}/runs` | `{model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` → `{run_id}` (202) |
325	| `POST /runs` | `{scenario_id, model?, seed?, max_steps?}` → creates a conversation then a run → `{run_id, conversation_id}` |
326	| `GET /runs/{id}` | `RunRecord` (record + all events + evaluation + usage) |
327	| `GET /runs/{id}/events` | SSE; `id:` = `seq`; honours `Last-Event-ID`; server closes at ~110 s |
328	
329	Ownership: every `/conversations/*` and `/runs/*` route resolves the resource, compares `user_id` with
330	the header, and returns 404 on mismatch (not 403 — no existence oracle).
331	
332	Wire shapes (JSON; the pydantic models in `faultline_common.schemas` and `apps/web/src/lib/types.ts`
333	mirror these field for field; DB columns `input_json` / `fault_json` / `evaluation_json` are parsed
334	into `input` / `fault` / `evaluation` on the wire):
335	
336	```ts
337	type User                = { id: string; created_at: string; last_seen_at: string }
338	type Conversation        = { id: string; user_id: string; scenario_id: string; title: string;
339	                             created_at: string; updated_at: string; archived_at: string | null }
340	type ConversationSummary = { id: string; title: string; scenario_id: string; created_at: string; updated_at: string;
341	                             last_run: { run_id: string; status: RunStatus; score: number | null } | null }
342	type Block               = { id: string; seq: number; type: 'text' | 'thinking' | 'tool_use' | 'tool_result';
343	                             text?: string | null; tool_name?: string | null; tool_use_id?: string | null;
344	                             input?: Record<string, unknown> | null; is_error?: boolean | null; exit_code?: number | null;
345	                             duration_ms?: number | null; fault?: FaultFired | null; truncated: boolean }
346	type Message             = { id: string; conversation_id: string; run_id: string; seq: number;
347	                             role: 'user' | 'assistant'; step?: number | null; created_at: string; blocks: Block[] }
348	type LlmCall             = { id: string; run_id: string; step: number; attempt: number; model: string;
349	                             request_id?: string | null; stop_reason?: string | null;
350	                             input_tokens?: number | null; output_tokens?: number | null;
351	                             cache_read_tokens?: number | null; cache_write_tokens?: number | null;
352	                             started_at: string; duration_ms?: number | null; error?: string | null }
353	type RunSummary          = { run_id: string; status: RunStatus; scenario_id: string; model: string;
354	                             created_at: string; finished_at: string | null; events: number; score: number | null }
355	// RunRecord gains three optional fields: conversation_id?, user_id?, task_prompt?
356	// Event.data for the two new types:
357	//   'llm.call'      { attempt, model, stop_reason, usage: { input_tokens, output_tokens,
358	//                     cache_read_input_tokens?, cache_creation_input_tokens? }, request_id?, duration_ms }
359	//   'turn.thinking' { text }
360	// Responses:
361	//   GET  /me                        → { user_id: string; conversations: number }
362	//   GET  /conversations             → ConversationSummary[]
363	//   POST /conversations             → Conversation
364	//   GET  /conversations/{id}        → { conversation: Conversation; runs: RunSummary[]; messages: Message[] }
365	//   PATCH /conversations/{id}       → Conversation          DELETE → { archived: true }
366	//   POST /conversations/{id}/runs   → { run_id: string; conversation_id: string }
367	//   POST /runs                      → { run_id: string; conversation_id?: string }   (conversation_id once Store lands)
368	```
369	
370	Until the Store lands, the web app sends `X-Faultline-User` anyway and treats 404 on `/me` and
371	`/conversations` as "history unavailable" (rail hidden, runs still work through `POST /runs`).
372	
373	### 5.2 Sandbox-env (gym + MCP)
374	
375	Unchanged from `PLAN.md` §2.2: REST `POST /episodes` (reset), `GET /episodes/{id}` (observe),
376	`POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate`, `DELETE /episodes/{id}`, `GET /scenarios`, `GET /health`; MCP tools
377	`run_command`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir` at `/mcp`, episode selected by the
378	`X-Faultline-Episode` header so the model can never name another episode.
379	
380	### 5.3 Event stream
381	
382	Event types and payloads are defined in `faultline_common.schemas.Event` (`run.started`, `episode.reset`,
383	`turn.thinking`, `turn.text`, `tool.call`, `tool.result`, `llm.call`, `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`,
384	`episode.evaluated`, `run.finished`, `log`). `Event.id` is the 0-based sequence within the run and doubles
385	as the SSE id, the `events.seq` primary key, and the resume cursor. Events are produced by exactly one
386	writer per run (`run_episode`) and are never edited.
387	
388	### 5.4 Reliability event contract (confirmed with the harness lead 2026-09-12 ~22:45 EDT)
389	
390	Canonical: `packages/common/faultline_common/schemas.py` (the `Event` docstring lists every data
391	shape), labels in `docs/error-taxonomy.md`, injector behaviour in `services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md`.
392	The UI is a ten-second reliability read — *task → fault (simulated or real, which layer) → did the
393	command execute (executed / failed / not executed / unknown are four different answers) → recovery
394	(agent actions vs harness retries; did the sandbox survive) → diff + independent checks* — and it
395	**never derives an outcome from error text**: anything absent renders as "unknown" with the raw
396	payload expandable. Fields (all additive):
397	
398	| Where | Field | Meaning |
399	|---|---|---|
400	| `tool.result.data` | `outcome: executed \| failed \| not_executed \| unknown` | executed = ran and succeeded; failed = ran and reported failure; not_executed = refused/short-circuited before the sandbox saw it; unknown = may or may not have run (ack lost, transport, worker interrupted) |
401	| `tool.result.data` | `error_class` (iff `is_error`): `{origin: injected\|staged\|real, layer: boundary\|filesystem\|sandbox\|transport\|harness\|model\|gym, code, kind?, label, outcome_known, side_effect_applied?, detail?}` | provenance and layer; `label` is a fixed string rendered verbatim; `error_code` kept for compatibility; raw text stays in `output` |
402	| `tool.result.data` | `attempts`, `sandbox: {id, alive}` | harness-level retries of this call (1 = none); identity/liveness of the workspace that answered |
403	| `fault.fired.data` | `origin` (injected \| staged, never real), `layer` (boundary for injected, filesystem for staged), `description` | one plain sentence per fault |
404	| `episode.reset.data` | `sandbox_id` (short), `attempt` | workspace identity; >1 when re-provisioned |
405	| `episode.sandbox` (new) | `{sandbox_id, status: alive\|terminated\|replaced, reason, step}` | the environment noticed the worker changed |
406	| `interruption` (new) | `Interruption {step?, layer, code, label, tool_use_id?, tool?, path?, outcome_known, planned, resumed, worker_generation, at, detail?}` | always real; `planned: true` when a scenario `harness_faults` entry triggered it |
407	| `run.resumed` (new) | `{worker_generation, resumed_from_event_id, dangling_tool_use_id?, resumed_at}` | a fresh worker picked the run up |
408	| `run.finished.data` | `evaluation_status: ok\|failed\|skipped`, `evaluation_error?`, `steps`, `score?` | a run that could not be graded is `unevaluated`, never "ok" |
409	| `RunStatus` | `+ unevaluated`, `+ interrupted` | "ok" now implies a score |
410	| `RunRecord` | `error_class?`, `interruptions[]`, `worker_generation` | why the run ended as it did |
411	| `GET /scenarios` | `checks: [{id, description, weight}]`, `faults_public: [{kind, origin, layer, description}]`, `harness_faults: [{kind: worker_crash\|transport_abort, tool, path, nth, after_ms}]` | intended outcome and fault classes known before the run; the agent never sees them |
412	| codes (final) | `ENOENT EACCES ETIMEDOUT EINVAL ESANDBOX ETRANSPORT EHARNESS EMODEL EGYM EINTERNAL ENOEPISODE` | `ETIMEDOUT` is ack-lost *or* a real client timeout — origin/layer tell them apart |
413	
414	Older records (e.g. `r_e9bc5c8c6739`) predate these fields; the UI resolves what it can from the
415	evaluate ledger (`outcome: ack_lost` ⇒ the write landed, `short_circuit` ⇒ nothing ran) and says
416	"unknown" otherwise. Web-side model: `apps/web/src/lib/reliability.ts` (`buildVerdict`), rendered by
417	`src/components/verdict/VerdictStrip.tsx`.
418	
419	## 6. Identity
Assistant2026-09-12T23:18:56.914Z
⚙ Read
{
  "file_path": "/Users/sohamwaychal/Documents/anthropic/ARCHITECTURE.md",
  "offset": 419,
  "limit": 150
}
Tool reply2026-09-12T23:18:56.925Z
419	## 6. Identity
420	
421	- **Minting.** `src/lib/identity.ts`: read `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]`, else the `faultline_uid`
422	  cookie, else mint `"u_" + crypto.randomUUID()`; write back to both. Cookie attributes:
423	  `SameSite=Lax; Secure; Max-Age=31536000; Path=/`. Not `HttpOnly` (JS writes it). All storage access is
424	  wrapped in try/catch; a browser that blocks storage still works for the session with an in-memory id.
425	- **Transport.** The harness API lives on a different origin from the SPA, so cookies are never sent to
426	  it. The id travels as `X-Faultline-User` on every request; the harness CORS config allows that header.
427	- **Why both stores.** localStorage is the primary (no size/attribute quirks, not sent anywhere);
428	  the cookie only exists so the id survives a "clear site data → local storage" that leaves cookies.
429	- **Threat model.** This is scoping, not authentication (explicit non-goal). Ids are 122-bit random and
430	  never listed, so they are unguessable in practice, but anyone holding one can read that browser's
431	  transcripts. Nothing sensitive is stored: scenarios are bundled, the workspace is a fixture, and the
432	  provider key never leaves `run_episode`. `POST …/runs` is rate-limited per id.
433	
434	## 7. Web application
435	
436	### 7.1 Stack
437	
438	Vite 8 + React 19 + TypeScript 6, Tailwind v4 (`@tailwindcss/vite`), shadcn v4 CLI (style `base-nova`
439	on Base UI, `neutral` base colour, CSS variables, the `cn` helper package, lucide icons) for primitives,
440	Beautiful UI for the AI-native composites, vitest (jsdom). No client router: the URL carries
441	`?c=<conversation_id>`, `?run=<run_id>` or `?demo=<scenario_id>`. **Node ≥ 22.12 is required** (Vite 8
442	`^20.19 || >=22.12`, vitest 5 `^22.12 || ^24`; `.nvmrc` pins 22 and the Modal image installs Node 22).
443	Built into `dist/` inside the Modal image and served by a Modal Server (`python -m http.server`, or
444	`@modal.web_server` as the fallback), with `VITE_HARNESS_URL` baked in and `/config.json` as a runtime
445	override (precedence: injected `window.__FAULTLINE_CONFIG__` → `/config.json` → build env → default) so
446	the harness URL can change without a rebuild.
447	
448	### 7.2 Beautiful UI: what it is and how it is integrated
449	
450	`https://www.beautifului.dev` is an MIT-licensed gallery (© 2026 Shane Levine) of 21 React + Tailwind
451	components "for AI-native interfaces". It is **not** an npm package and **not** a shadcn registry: each
452	component has a *Copy code* button, and the TSX source is embedded in the page. Integration is therefore
453	copy-paste into `src/components/bui/` with a LICENSE file, plus three adaptations that the source makes
454	necessary:
455	
456	| Adaptation | Reason |
457	|---|---|
458	| icons → `lucide-react` | `SidebarNav` imports twelve icons from `@central-icons-react/*`, a paid set |
459	| unpublished atoms → shadcn | components import `@/components/atoms/{Button,StreamText,Shimmer,ValuePill,EntityChip}` and `@/components/primitives/GlideMenu`, which the gallery does not publish; shadcn `Button`, `Skeleton`, `Badge`, `DropdownMenu` stand in |
460	| drop `glimm` | `PromptBar` imports a shader/sound helper; the branch is deleted |
461	| `accent*` classes → `brand*` | shadcn already owns `accent` / `accent-foreground` (its subtle hover surface); Beautiful UI uses `accent` as the brand colour. Renaming on copy keeps both palettes intact |
462	
463	Components used and their role: `SidebarNav` (conversation rail), `PromptBar` (composer with scenario
464	`/` command and model picker), `StreamingText` (assistant prose), `ThinkingState` (per-step trace),
465	`ToolChips` (tool calls with output/exit code/duration/fault badge), `CodeBlock` (outputs and unified
466	diffs), `TaskRows` (score card), `LoadingState`, `DiffTable` (workspace file status). No animation library
467	is required; the components use CSS transitions and `useLayoutEffect`.
468	
469	Theme bridge: Beautiful UI is written against its own Tailwind tokens (`ink`, `ink-2`, `ink-3`, `canvas`,
470	`surface`, `line`, `line-strong`, and `accent`, `accent-tint`, `accent-ink` → renamed `brand*`). They are
471	defined once in the existing `@theme inline` block of `src/index.css` as aliases of shadcn's CSS
472	variables (`--foreground`, `--muted-foreground`, `--background`, `--card`, `--border`, `--input`,
473	`--primary`, `--primary-foreground`), so both libraries follow the same light/dark palette (exact block
474	in `PLAN.md` §2.10.2).
475	
476	### 7.3 Layout, routes, state
477	
478	```
479	┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
480	│ SidebarNav   │ header: title · scenario · model · status │ Workspace (Tabs)     │
481	│  + New run   │           step n/max · tokens · elapsed   │  Files (DiffTable)   │
482	│  search      │ ─────────────────────────────────────────│  Diffs (CodeBlock)   │
483	│  today       │ [user] task prompt                        │  Ledger              │
484	│   · lost-ack │ [assistant] StreamingText                 │                      │
485	│   · locked…  │    ThinkingState ▸ ToolChips (CodeBlock)  │                      │
486	│  yesterday   │      fault ⚡ · recovered ✓               │                      │
487	│   · …        │ [assistant] summary + TaskRows score card │                      │
488	│              │ ─────────────────────────────────────────│                      │
489	│ u_1a2b… ·    │ PromptBar  /scenario  model ▾  seed  Run  │                      │
490	│ Replay demo  │                                           │                      │
491	└──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
492	```
493	
494	- Landing (`/`): five bullets explaining the app, a centered composer (`/` picks a scenario, model and
495	  seed chips), and below it a wide shadcn data table of scenarios (TanStack Table v9, fixed layout)
496	  with separate columns for title, description, max turns, failure badges, Live run and Replay;
497	  every injected-fault scenario ships a real recorded replay; harness reachability is a composer hint
498	  and a line in the identity menu, not a card.
499	- URL state (no router, as scaffolded): no params = the landing page;
500	  `?c=<conversation_id>` = a persisted transcript; `?c=…&run=<run_id>` = that run inside its
501	  conversation (live tail if unfinished); `?demo=<scenario_id>` = bundled replay.
502	- One pure reducer (`src/lib/reducer.ts`, already present) builds the view model for live SSE, for
503	  `GET /runs/{id}` restore, and for replay of `public/demo/*.json`. Persisted transcripts from
504	  `GET /conversations/{id}` are mapped to the same view model, so every path renders through the same
505	  components.
506	- `src/lib/api.ts` (already present) reconnects SSE with `Last-Event-ID` and, after two failed opens,
507	  falls back to polling `GET /runs/{id}`; identity is added to it as a default header.
508	- Theme: light / dark / system, chosen from a radio group of icon buttons in the user menu (bottom-left
509	  sidebar footer) and stored
510	  per browser (`faultline.theme`); "system" follows `prefers-color-scheme` live; a one-off
511	  `?theme=light|dark|system` in the URL sets and persists the choice (for sharing a link in a given
512	  look, and for screenshot tooling). The choice is applied
513	  by toggling the `dark` class on `<html>` (shadcn's dark variant) both from an inline pre-paint script
514	  in `index.html` and from `src/lib/theme.ts`, so the first paint is already right. Semantic colour
515	  tones are written as light/dark pairs so status badges keep contrast on both backgrounds.
516	- Workspace panel: shadcn Resizable panels (`react-resizable-panels` 4.x) on ≥1280 px — draggable
517	  handle, collapsible to zero, split persisted in localStorage, the top-bar toggle bound to the
518	  panel's imperative handle; an overlay sheet below that width. The Logs tab virtualizes its rows
519	  (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, rows measured after render because lines wrap) and follows the tail
520	  while a run is live.
521	- Verdict strip (`src/components/verdict/VerdictStrip.tsx`, model in `src/lib/reliability.ts`): the
522	  five-panel ten-second read described in §5.4, rendered above every transcript (live, persisted,
523	  replay); details and raw JSON expand per panel; the sandbox chip in the top bar shows workspace
524	  identity, worker generation and survival.
525	- Unified logging mirrors the server's JSON shape to the console with `svc:"web"`.
526	
527	## 8. Deployment and configuration
528	
529	| Item | Value |
530	|---|---|
531	| Workspace / environment | `appliedlabsai` / `local` → web functions at `https://appliedlabsai-local--<app>-<fn>.modal.run`; the static site is a Modal **Server**, whose URL is `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-web-site.us-east.modal.direct` (read it back with `modal.Server.from_name("faultline-web", "site").get_url()`; the `.modal.run` shape answers 404 for Servers) |
532	| Deploy order | sandbox-env → harness (creates Volume `faultline-db` on first deploy) → web. Live URLs (2026-09-12): harness `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-harness-api.modal.run`, sandbox-env `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-sandbox-env-api.modal.run`, web `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-web-site.us-east.modal.direct` |
533	| Secret | `anthropic-secret` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, optional `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE`; name overridable via `FAULTLINE_ANTHROPIC_SECRET`), attached only to `run_episode` |
534	| Volume | `faultline-db` (v1), mounted at `/data` on `Store` only |
535	| Env | `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` (default `claude-haiku-4-5`), `SANDBOX_ENV_URL`, `HARNESS_URL`, `LOG_LEVEL` (see `.env.example`) |
536	| Warm containers | web Server, harness `api`, harness `Store` (`min_containers=1`) |
537	| Web image | Node 22 + pnpm; `pnpm i --frozen-lockfile && pnpm build`; `config.json` written from `HARNESS_URL` at container start |
538	| Sandbox guardrails | `timeout=1800`, `idle_timeout=600`, `block_network=True`, delete in `finally`, `reap` helper |
539	| Export | `modal run … ::checkpoint_now` then `modal volume get faultline-db faultline.sqlite3 runs/` |
540	
541	## 9. Decision log
542	
543	| # | Decision | Alternatives considered | Why |
544	|---|---|---|---|
545	| 1 | Three Modal apps with a strict secret boundary | one app | harness swappable, environment gradeable without trusting the agent, static UI; the provider key exists in exactly one function |
546	| 2 | Faults injected at the tool boundary, not inside the shell | `chmod`, deleting files live, network faults | deterministic, seedable, invisible to the agent, gradeable from a ledger; ack-lost is only expressible at the boundary |
547	| 3 | `spawn` + event log + reconnecting SSE | run the loop inside the request | Modal web requests cap at 150 s |
548	| 4 | Persistence = SQLite on a Modal Volume | Modal Dict only; hosted Postgres; Modal Dict as hot path + SQLite archive | user asked for it; zero external services; a single file is trivial to export as evidence |
549	| 5 | Single-writer `Store` actor (`max_containers=1`) | every function opens the DB on the Volume; per-run DB files | Volumes have no distributed locking and last-write-wins; one process owning the file removes every consistency question |
550	| 6 | Hot DB on local disk, `VACUUM INTO` snapshots renamed atomically onto the Volume | run SQLite directly on the FUSE mount | WAL/locking on FUSE is undocumented, and background commits are not multi-file snapshots; Modal's own example copies-then-commits |
551	| 7 | Volume v1 | v2 | v2 needs an explicit flag and is marked experimental in the 1.5.5 CLI; no benefit for one file |
552	| 8 | `events` as source of truth + `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` projections | events only; projections only | replay/SSE need the log; the UI and "what did the model see" need the message shape; projections are a cheap same-transaction write and a listed scope cut |
553	| 9 | Identity via `X-Faultline-User` header, minted in the browser, mirrored to a cookie | cookie-only; server-minted id | the API is cross-origin so cookies never reach it; localStorage is the reliable store; the cookie is a backup |
554	| 10 | Beautiful UI by copy-paste with lucide/shadcn substitutions | Vercel AI Elements (a real registry); shadcn only | matches the requested look; MIT; the private deps are small and swappable; scope cut #4 if it drags |
555	| 11 | Haiku 4.5 default, Sonnet/Opus 5 allowlisted | Sonnet default | cost and latency for a demo; the ack-lost scenario is more interesting when a cheaper model sometimes fails it |
556	| 12 | No client router; conversation/run/demo in query params | `react-router-dom` | the scaffold already does this and it is enough for three screens; one less dependency in a static SPA |
557	| 13 | Node 22 pinned (`.nvmrc`, `engines`, Modal image) | stay on the machine default 21.6 | Vite 8 and vitest 5 refuse to start on 21.6 (`styleText` missing from `node:util`) |
558	
559	## 10. Open questions
560	
561	- Should the composer allow editing the task prompt, or only choosing a scenario? Current answer: editable,
562	  stored in `runs.task_prompt`, because it makes the "conversation" framing honest. Revisit if it invites
563	  prompt-injection-shaped demos that distract from the reliability story.
564	- Do we need per-user rate limiting beyond `POST …/runs`? Probably not for review traffic.
565	- Checkpoint cadence (15 s) vs. cost of `VACUUM INTO` as the DB grows — measure once real runs exist and
566	  record the number in `PLAN.md` §0.

1	# Faultline — living plan
2	
3	> **Faultline** is a mini agent harness with a browser view. A Claude agent runs *real* shell
4	> commands (read files, edit code, run tests) inside an isolated Modal Sandbox. The environment
5	> deliberately injects failures — a missing file, a denied write, a write that lands but whose
6	> response times out — and the UI shows every command, its output, the file changes, and whether
7	> the agent recovered. It is framed as a tiny RL gym (`reset / step / observe / evaluate`) so the
8	> same environment can grade recovery behaviour, not just task completion.
9	>
10	> Assignment: Anthropic Platform SWE take-home. Themes: **3 Systems & Reliability** (primary) and
11	> **4 Evaluation** (secondary). Target 1–2h, hard limit 8h. Hosted prototype + repo + rationale
12	> (video + doc) + AI transcripts. **Track time in §9.**

35	
36	## 1. Goals, non-goals, done condition
37	
38	**Goal.** A reviewer opens a URL, picks a scenario, presses *Run*, and watches a Claude agent
39	recover (or not) from injected faults while editing a small Python repo, with a graded result.
40	No local install for the reviewer. Works even if the live model call fails (bundled replay).
41	
42	**Done condition (whole project).**
43	- [ ] Three Modal apps deployed in env `local` and reachable: `faultline-web`, `faultline-harness`, `faultline-sandbox-env`.
44	- [ ] A live run of every bundled scenario completes end-to-end in the browser with a score card.
45	- [ ] Evidence for ≥1 run per scenario saved under `runs/` (gitignored) and one exported to `apps/web/public/demo/` for replay.
46	- [ ] Conversations, messages and LLM turns persist in SQLite on a Modal Volume: they survive a page reload, a browser restart (same browser) and a redeploy of the harness (§2.9).
47	- [ ] `README.md` explains architecture + how to run; `RATIONALE.md` written; video recorded; transcripts exported.
48	
49	**Non-goals (explicitly out).** Real authentication (identity is an anonymous browser id — a
50	scoping convenience, not a security boundary, §2.9.1), GPU anything, arbitrary user repos, streaming
51	model tokens (we stream *events*, not tokens), RL training, free-form chat with the agent (a
52	conversation is a thread of runs; the user's message *is* the task prompt).
53	
54	---
55	
56	## 2. Design (decisions + research notes)
57	
58	### 2.1 Architecture and trust boundaries
59	
60	```
61	 browser (apps/web: Vite + shadcn + Beautiful UI, static on Modal Server; anonymous user id in localStorage + cookie mirror)
62	    │  HTTPS JSON + SSE (reconnecting, Last-Event-ID); every request carries X-Faultline-User
63	    ▼
64	 services/agent-harness  (Modal app "faultline-harness")
65	    ├─ api          : FastAPI @modal.asgi_app — /me, /conversations…, /runs…, GET /runs/{id}/events (SSE), /scenarios, /health
66	    │                 NO provider secret. Identifies the browser by X-Faultline-User. Spawns run_episode; reads/writes ONLY via Store.
67	    ├─ Store        : @app.cls(max_containers=1, volumes={"/data": faultline-db}) — the single SQLite writer (§2.9).
68	    │                 users / conversations / runs / events / messages / blocks / llm_calls. Hot DB on local disk,
69	    │                 consistent snapshots checkpointed to the Modal Volume. No secret, no fault plan.
70	    └─ run_episode  : @app.function(secrets=[anthropic-secret]) — the ONLY code that sees ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
71	                      Owns the model loop (anthropic SDK, model from ANTHROPIC_MODEL / per-run override).
72	                      Talks to sandbox-env over MCP (streamable HTTP) + gym REST. Appends events to Store.
73	    │  HTTPS: gym REST (reset/observe/evaluate) + MCP /mcp (step)
74	    ▼
75	 services/sandbox-env  (Modal app "faultline-sandbox-env")   ← controller, fault plan, grader live HERE
76	    └─ api : FastAPI @modal.asgi_app with FastMCP mounted at /mcp. Stateless containers; episode state in
77	             modal.Dict "faultline-episodes" {sandbox_id, scenario, seed, fault_plan, ledger, baseline}.
78	             Every tool call: load episode → consult fault plan → (maybe) exec in the Modal Sandbox → record ledger.
79	    │  modal.Sandbox.from_id(sid).exec(...)   (block_network=True, no secrets, per-episode)
80	    ▼
81	 Modal Sandbox (the *command sandbox*): fixture repo at /workspace, python3 + pytest. Sees nothing else.
82	```
83	
84	Trust boundary summary (what each process can see):
85	
86	| Process | Provider key | Fault plan / grader | Workspace files | Network |
87	|---|---|---|---|---|
88	| browser | ✗ | ✗ (sees *events* about faults after the fact) | via observe API | harness only |
89	| harness `api` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | Store + spawn |
90	| harness `Store` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ (only what events carry: diffs, outputs) | Volume only |
91	| harness `run_episode` | ✓ (secret `anthropic-secret`: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, optional `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE`) | ✗ | via MCP only | Anthropic + sandbox-env |
92	| sandbox-env `api` | ✗ | ✓ | via Sandbox API | Modal Sandbox only |
93	| Modal Sandbox (agent's shell) | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ | **none** (`block_network=True`) |
94	
95	Why three deployables: the harness must be replaceable (different model/prompt) without touching
96	the environment; the environment must be gradeable without trusting the agent; the UI is static.
97	
98	### 2.2 Gym contract (sandbox-env)
99	
100	Gymnasium-style, but the *step* surface is MCP tools (so any MCP-speaking harness can play).
101	
102	REST (JSON) on `https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-sandbox-env-api.modal.run`:
103	
104	| Route | Purpose | Body → Response |
105	|---|---|---|
106	| `POST /episodes` (**reset**) | provision sandbox, copy fixture, apply sticky faults, store plan | `{scenario_id, seed?}` → `{episode_id, scenario: {id,title,task_prompt,max_steps}, workspace_root, files: [...]}` |
107	| `GET /episodes/{id}` (**observe**) | tree + diff vs baseline + public ledger (faults *fired* so far, without revealing pending ones) | → `{episode_id, step, files:[{path,size,sha,status:added/modified/deleted/unchanged}], diffs:[{path,unified}], faults_fired:[...], done}` |
108	| `POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate` | grader: upload hidden tests into sandbox, run pytest, remove; compute recovery metrics from ledger | → `{score, passed, checks:[{id,ok,detail}], tests:{passed,failed,output}, ledger:[...]}` |
109	| `DELETE /episodes/{id}` | terminate sandbox | → `{terminated:true}` |
110	| `GET /scenarios` | bundled scenario catalogue (public fields only) | |
111	| `GET /health` | | |
112	
113	MCP (streamable HTTP, stateless) at `/mcp`; episode selected by header `X-Faultline-Episode: <episode_id>`
114	(middleware → contextvar; tools never take the id as an argument so the model can't spoof another episode).
115	
116	| Tool | Input | Output (text + structured) | Faults that can apply |
117	|---|---|---|---|
118	| `run_command` | `{command: str, timeout_s?: int≤60}` | `{stdout, stderr, exit_code, duration_ms, truncated}` | `missing_file` (argv token match), `denied_write` (argv token match on mutating verbs) |
119	| `read_file` | `{path}` | `{content, size, sha256}` or ENOENT | `missing_file` |
120	| `write_file` | `{path, content, mode: "overwrite"\|"append"}` | `{bytes_written, sha256}` | `denied_write`, `ack_lost` |
121	| `list_dir` | `{path?}` | `{entries:[{name,type,size}]}` | `missing_file` (dir) |
122	
123	Every tool result is capped (`stdout`/`stderr` 8 KB each, `content` 32 KB) and marked `truncated`.
124	Every call is appended to the **ledger**: `{step, tool, args_digest, fault?:{kind,path,mode}, outcome, ts, duration_ms}`.
125	
126	**Fault plan schema** (per scenario, seeded; lives only in the Dict, never in the sandbox):
127	
128	```json
129	{"faults": [
130	  {"kind": "missing_file", "path": "config/settings.json", "mode": "sticky",    "hits": null},
131	  {"kind": "missing_file", "path": "README.md",            "mode": "transient", "hits": 1},
132	  {"kind": "denied_write", "path": "src/limits.py",        "mode": "transient", "hits": 2},
133	  {"kind": "ack_lost",     "path": "CHANGELOG.md",         "mode": "transient", "hits": 1, "delay_ms": 3000}
134	]}
135	```
136	
137	How each fault is realised **at the tool boundary** (sandbox-env, outside the shell):
138	
139	| Kind | Reset | On matching call | What the agent sees | Recovery we grade |
140	|---|---|---|---|---|
141	| `missing_file` sticky | file deleted from fixture before agent starts | — | `ENOENT` from cat/read | agent recreates it from the spec in README/tests; hidden tests pass |
142	| `missing_file` transient | nothing | first `hits` reads short-circuit with ENOENT (no exec) | flaky FS | agent retries/lists dir instead of giving up |
143	| `denied_write` | nothing | first `hits` writes short-circuit with `EACCES: Permission denied`; **write not performed** | locked file | agent retries; final content correct; ≤1 wasted retry |
144	| `ack_lost` | nothing | **write IS performed**, then sleep `delay_ms`, then return `is_error` "504: response timed out; operation may or may not have completed" | ambiguous timeout | agent **verifies** (read/cat/grep/sha) before retrying; **no duplicate append**; tests pass |
145	
146	**Score** = 60 · tests_pass + 40 · recovery (checks weighted equally per scenario), reported with a
147	per-check breakdown. Step budget `max_steps` (default 20) → `truncated` if exceeded.
148	
149	### 2.3 Harness loop (agent-harness/run_episode)
150	
151	- SDK: `anthropic` (Python). Client: `anthropic.Anthropic()` (reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from the secret).
152	- Model: `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` env (default `claude-haiku-4-5`), per-run override from an allowlist
153	  (`claude-haiku-4-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-opus-5`). Thinking: adaptive on models that support it; Haiku 4.5 = off.
154	- Tools: discovered from MCP `list_tools` and converted to Anthropic tool defs (+ a local `submit` tool
155	  that ends the episode with a summary). Parallel tool calls executed in order, all results returned in one user message.
156	- Loop: manual `while stop_reason == "tool_use"` (no beta dependency), `max_steps`, per-call timeouts,
157	  typed error chain (`RateLimitError` → retry w/ backoff; `APIStatusError ≥500` → retry; else fail the run cleanly).
158	- System prompt strategy (recovery-oriented): one command per step, verify state after any error,
159	  after a timeout **read before re-writing**, prefer idempotent edits, run tests before submitting.
160	- Events appended to the Store (`Store().append_events.remote(run_id, [events])`, batched per step,
161	  idempotent on `(run_id, seq)`) as they happen. The loop also keeps them in memory and re-sends the
162	  full list at `run.finished`, so a Store restart self-heals (§2.9):
163	
164	```
165	run.started {run_id, scenario, model, seed}
166	episode.reset {episode_id, files}
167	turn.text {step, text}                       # assistant prose
168	tool.call {step, tool, input, tool_use_id}
169	tool.result {step, tool_use_id, output, is_error, duration_ms, fault?}   # fault echoed by sandbox-env public ledger
170	llm.call {step, attempt, model, stop_reason, usage:{input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_input_tokens, cache_creation_input_tokens}, request_id, duration_ms}   # one per messages.create — the "LLM turn"
171	turn.thinking {step, text}                   # thinking block (summary) when adaptive thinking is on
172	fault.fired {step, kind, path, mode}         # emitted by harness after reading ledger delta
173	workspace.diff {step, files, diffs}          # from observe, after each mutating step
174	episode.evaluated {score, checks, tests}
175	run.finished {status: ok|error|truncated, usage:{input_tokens, output_tokens}, duration_ms}
176	log {level, msg, ...}                        # verbose unified log lines mirrored into the stream
177	```
178	
179	Why not run the loop inside the SSE request: Modal web endpoints cap requests at **150 s**
180	(303-redirect polling after that breaks CORS). So `POST /runs` → `run_episode.spawn(...)` and the
181	browser tails `GET /runs/{id}/events` via SSE (the `api` polls `Store.events_after(run_id, after_seq)`
182	every 400 ms); the server closes each stream at ~110 s and the browser reconnects with
183	`Last-Event-ID` (= `events.seq`). `GET /runs/{id}` returns the whole record (also what we save as
184	evidence).
185	
186	### 2.4 Modal deployment shapes (verified against modal 1.5.5 locally)
187	
188	- Workspace `appliedlabsai`, environment `local` (web suffix `local`) → URLs
189	  `https://appliedlabsai-local--<app>-<fn>.modal.run` (label truncation at 63 chars).
190	- sandbox-env: `@app.function(image=IMG, timeout=300) @modal.concurrent(max_inputs=20) @modal.asgi_app()` → `api`.
191	  Sandboxes: `modal.Sandbox.create(app=modal.App.lookup("faultline-sandboxes", create_if_missing=True), image=SANDBOX_IMG, workdir="/workspace", timeout=1800, idle_timeout=600, block_network=True, cpu=1, memory=1024)`;
192	  exec via `sb.exec("bash","-lc",cmd, timeout=…)`; files via `sb.filesystem.write_text/read_text` (verify names against 1.5.5).
193	- harness: `api` as above (no secrets); `run_episode` = `@app.function(image=IMG, secrets=[modal.Secret.from_name("anthropic-secret")], timeout=900)`.
194	  Env: `SANDBOX_ENV_URL` (deploy-time env or `modal.Function.from_name("faultline-sandbox-env","api").get_web_url()` at runtime).
195	- harness store: `vol = modal.Volume.from_name("faultline-db", create_if_missing=True)` (v1 — v2 needs an explicit
196	  `version=2` and the 1.5.5 CLI still flags it experimental; it buys nothing for one file).
197	  `@app.cls(image=IMG, volumes={"/data": vol}, max_containers=1, min_containers=1, timeout=60) @modal.concurrent(max_inputs=32) class Store`
198	  with `@modal.enter` (restore snapshot → `/tmp`, migrate), `@modal.exit` (checkpoint), one `@modal.method` per operation.
199	  Same-app callers: `await Store().events_after.remote.aio(...)` from `api`, `Store().append_events.remote(...)` from `run_episode`.
200	  Local entrypoint `checkpoint_now` forces a snapshot for evidence/export (§5).
201	- web: `@app.server(image=WEB_IMG, unauthenticated=True, port=8000, min_containers=1)` class with `@modal.enter` → `python -m http.server -d /site 8000`.
202	  Image builds the Vite app: **Node 22** (Vite 8 needs `^20.19 || >=22.12`, vitest 5 needs `^22.12 || ^24`; `.nvmrc`=22) + pnpm installed in image, `add_local_dir("apps/web", copy=True)`, `run_commands("pnpm i --frozen-lockfile && pnpm build")` with `VITE_HARNESS_URL` baked in **and** a runtime `config.json` fallback so the URL can change without a rebuild.
203	  Fallback if Server misbehaves: `@modal.web_server(8000)`.
204	- Deploy order: sandbox-env → harness → web. Commands in §5.
205	- Secret: `anthropic-secret` (keys `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, optional `ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE`), env `local`. Attached **only** to `run_episode`.
206	  Guard: `run_episode` logs `has_key=True/False` and `workspace=<masked>`; `api` asserts the key is *absent* in its env at startup.
207	
208	### 2.5 Unified logging
209	
210	One line per event, JSON, to stdout in every service (Modal captures → `modal app logs <app> -e local`),
211	mirrored to the browser console in the web app with the same shape:
212	
213	```
214	{"ts":"2026-09-12T14:03:22.114Z","svc":"sandbox-env","lvl":"info","run_id":"r_…","episode_id":"ep_…","step":4,"ev":"tool.result","tool":"write_file","fault":"ack_lost","dur_ms":3041,"msg":"write landed; ack withheld"}
215	```
216	
217	Fields: `ts svc lvl run_id episode_id step ev msg` + free extras. `LOG_LEVEL=debug` turns on
218	argument/output dumps (truncated). Shared implementation in `packages/common/faultline_common/log.py`
219	(added to each image via `add_local_python_source`).

259	### 2.9 Persistence: SQLite on a Modal Volume (harness `Store`)
260	
261	Full write-up, DDL and decision log: `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4. This section is the plan-level summary.
262	
263	**What is persisted.** The browser's anonymous identity, its conversations, every run, the append-only
264	event log of each run, and a transcript projection (messages → content blocks, plus one `llm_calls`
265	row per `messages.create`). Episode state (fault plan, ledger) stays in sandbox-env's
266	`modal.Dict "faultline-episodes"` — ephemeral by design and never readable by the harness.
267	
268	**Today's code (17:41 EDT).** `harness/store.py` is `RunStore` over the Modal Dict `faultline-runs`:
269	one whole `RunRecord` per run, re-`put` on every event, plus a 200-entry index. `api.py` has
270	`/health /scenarios POST /runs GET /runs GET /runs/{id} GET /runs/{id}/events`, CORS `allow_headers=["*"]`.
271	Migration: keep the `RunStore` interface (`get / create / list_runs`) as a thin client over the new
272	`Store` class so `loop.py` and `api.py` change minimally; add `append_events` so the loop stops
273	re-persisting whole records; drop the Dict.
274	
275	**Modal facts that shape the design** (docs + `modal==1.5.5`, verified 2026-09-12):
276	- Volumes are "write-once, read-many"; writes become visible to other containers only after a commit
277	  (background commits every few seconds, plus explicit `.commit()`); other readers must `.reload()`.
278	- "Last write wins in case of concurrent modification of the same file"; no distributed file locking.
279	- Modal's own SQLite example (Datasette) builds the DB on local disk, copies it onto the Volume, commits.
280	- Volume v2 needs explicit `version=2`; the 1.5.5 CLI marks it experimental → v1.
281	
282	**Decision — one writer, hot copy on local disk, consistent snapshots on the Volume.**
283	- `Store` = Modal class, `max_containers=1` (single writer; the only process that ever opens the DB),
284	  `min_containers=1` (no cold start per SSE poll). `@modal.concurrent(max_inputs=32)` + a
285	  `threading.Lock` around the connection; ops are sub-millisecond on local disk.
286	- DB at `/tmp/faultline.sqlite3` (`journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`) —
287	  plain SQLite semantics, no FUSE locking/mmap questions.
288	- Checkpoint = `VACUUM INTO '/data/faultline.sqlite3.tmp'` → `os.replace` → `vol.commit()`. Always a
289	  complete, consistent single file (a background commit can never capture a half-written DB).
290	  Triggered after every `run.finished`, every 15 s while dirty, and in `@modal.exit`.
291	  `@modal.enter` restores `/data/faultline.sqlite3 → /tmp` (or creates an empty DB) and runs
292	  migrations (`PRAGMA user_version`, `harness/migrations/000N_*.sql`).
293	- Loss window: ≤ the last 15 s of an *in-flight* run if the Store container dies. Completed runs
294	  self-heal: `append_events` is idempotent on `(run_id, seq)` and `run_episode` re-sends its full
295	  in-memory event list at `run.finished`.
296	- Nobody else touches the file. `api` and `run_episode` call `Store().<method>.remote(...)`; reads need
297	  no `reload()` because the reader *is* the writer.
298	- Accepted limit: one Store container, full-file snapshot per checkpoint. Past ~100 MB move to
299	  incremental shipping (Litestream-style / `modal-mosql`) or Postgres. Not a take-home problem.
300	
301	#### 2.9.1 Identity (browser → `X-Faultline-User`)
302	- First visit: `id = "u_" + crypto.randomUUID()` → `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]` + cookie mirror
303	  `faultline_uid` (`SameSite=Lax; Secure; Max-Age=31536000`). Read order: localStorage → cookie → mint.
304	  Both live on the web origin; the cookie only exists to survive a localStorage wipe.
305	- The harness API is a different origin (`…-harness-api.modal.run`), so cookies never reach it
306	  (`api.ts` already sends `credentials: 'omit'`). Identity travels as the `X-Faultline-User` header on
307	  every request (CORS already allows `*` headers).
308	- Server: validate `^u_[0-9a-f-]{36}$`, upsert `users` (`last_seen_at`, `user_agent`), scope every
309	  conversation/run route to it; mismatch → 404. Scoping, not auth (§1 non-goal).
310	
311	#### 2.9.2 Data model (tables; DDL in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.4)
312	```
313	users 1─∞ conversations 1─∞ runs 1─∞ events            (append-only; source of truth; SSE id == seq)
314	                              runs 1─∞ messages 1─∞ blocks  (projection: Anthropic message/content-block shape)
315	                              runs 1─∞ llm_calls           (projection of llm.call: usage, stop_reason, request_id, attempt)
316	```
317	| Table | Key columns |
318	|---|---|
319	| `users` | `id` (u_…), `created_at`, `last_seen_at`, `user_agent` |
320	| `conversations` | `id` (c_…), `user_id`, `scenario_id`, `title`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `archived_at` |
321	| `runs` | `id` (r_…), `conversation_id`, `user_id`, `scenario_id`, `model`, `seed`, `max_steps`, `task_prompt`, `episode_id`, `status`, `score`, `evaluation_json`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `error`, `created_at`, `started_at`, `finished_at` |
322	| `events` | PK `(run_id, seq)`, `ts`, `type`, `step`, `data` JSON |
323	| `messages` | `id` (m_…), `conversation_id`, `run_id`, `seq` (unique per conversation), `role` user\|assistant, `step`, `created_at` |
324	| `blocks` | `id` (b_…), `message_id`, `seq`, `type` text\|thinking\|tool_use\|tool_result, `text`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `input_json`, `is_error`, `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `fault_json`, `truncated` |
325	| `llm_calls` | `id` (l_…), `run_id`, `step`, `attempt`, `model`, `request_id`, `stop_reason`, token counts ×4, `started_at`, `duration_ms`, `error` |
326	
327	#### 2.9.3 Event → transcript projection (inside `append_events`, same transaction)
328	| Event | Projection |
329	|---|---|
330	| `episode.reset` | user message (run's first) with one `text` block = `task_prompt` |
331	| `turn.thinking` / `turn.text` / `tool.call` (same `step`) | one assistant message per step; blocks in event order: `thinking`, `text`, `tool_use{tool_name, tool_use_id, input_json}` |
332	| `tool.result` (same `step`) | one user message per step (Anthropic wire shape) with `tool_result{tool_use_id, text=output, is_error, exit_code, duration_ms, fault_json}` |
333	| `llm.call` | `llm_calls` row |
334	| `episode.evaluated` | `runs.score`, `runs.evaluation_json`; assistant message with the submit summary |
335	| `run.started` / `run.finished` | `runs.status / started_at / finished_at / tokens / error`; `conversations.updated_at` |
336	| `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`, `log` | events only (the UI reads them from the event log) |
337	
338	The UI groups each `tool_result` under its `tool_use` by `tool_use_id`. The same rows rebuild the exact
339	`messages=[…]` array the model saw — that is what "LLM turns during tool calls" means here.
340	
341	#### 2.9.4 Harness API additions (all scoped by `X-Faultline-User`)
342	| Route | Purpose |
343	|---|---|
344	| `GET /me` | upsert user → `{user_id, conversations: n}` |
345	| `GET /conversations` | `[{id, title, scenario_id, updated_at, last_run:{id,status,score}}]`, newest first, archived excluded |
346	| `POST /conversations` `{scenario_id, title?}` | create; title defaults to the scenario title |
347	| `GET /conversations/{id}` | conversation + runs + messages + blocks (the transcript) |
348	| `PATCH /conversations/{id}` `{title?, archived?}` · `DELETE` | rename / archive (soft) |
349	| `POST /conversations/{id}/runs` `{model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` | create run (`queued`) + `run_episode.spawn` → `{run_id}` |
350	| `POST /runs` | kept: creates a conversation then a run → `{run_id, conversation_id}` (used by `run_episode_cli.py` and today's `Home`) |
351	| `GET /runs` · `GET /runs/{id}` · `GET /runs/{id}/events` (SSE) | unchanged shapes; backed by Store; `GET /runs` scoped to the user |
352

427	### 2.11 Failure provenance and real interruptions (added 2026-09-12 18:35 EDT)
428	
429	The agent keeps seeing OS/HTTP-style errors; everything else gets provenance. Canonical types in
430	`schemas.py` (`ErrorClass`, `ToolOutcome`, `Interruption`, `HarnessFault`, `InterruptionReport`,
431	run statuses `unevaluated` / `interrupted`), contract for the UI in `docs/error-taxonomy.md`,
432	truthful per-injector semantics in `services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md`.
433	
434	- **origin**: `injected` (short-circuited or ack-withheld at the boundary; nothing real failed) ·
435	  `staged` (the scenario really deleted the file at reset; the ENOENT is a real OS error) ·
436	  `real` (unplanned failure of sandbox / transport / harness / model / gym / filesystem).
437	- **outcome** per tool result: `executed | failed | not_executed | unknown`.
438	- **codes**: `ENOENT EACCES ETIMEDOUT EINVAL` (agent-facing, same text either way) +
439	  `ESANDBOX ETRANSPORT EHARNESS EMODEL EGYM EINTERNAL ENOEPISODE` (real only).
440	- **scenario `worker-crash`**: the harness worker really dies (`os._exit`) while its first
441	  changelog write is in flight; Modal retries `run_episode`; the new worker resumes from the
442	  persisted events, tells the gym (`POST /episodes/{id}/interruptions`) and hands the agent an
443	  `EHARNESS` result with outcome unknown. Grading reuses `verified_before_rewrite`.
444	- Real specimen that motivated this: run `r_ccda8780cbee` (sandbox killed by a concurrent `reap`)
445	  ended `ok` with `score: null` labelled `EINTERNAL` → must become `interrupted` / `ESANDBOX`.
446	- Checklist: [ ] sandbox-env provenance + `ESANDBOX` + staged `faults_fired` + interruptions route +
447	  scenario passthrough + safe `reap` · [ ] harness classification + statuses + resume + `worker_crash`
448	  / `transport_abort` · [ ] live proofs (`scripts/prove_interruptions.py`, worker-crash + lost-ack +
449	  real sandbox loss) · [ ] verify skill: `features/interruptions.md` + stage · [ ] web labels (other session).

Tool reply2026-09-12T23:19:04.442Z
1	# Error and status taxonomy (contract for the UI, the ledger and the evidence)
2	
3	Two questions must be answerable for every failure a run shows: **who caused it** (`origin`) and
4	**which layer really failed** (`layer`). The agent never gets these; the UI always does.
5	
6	## `ErrorClass` (on every `tool.result` with `is_error`, on `fault.fired`, on `RunRecord.error_class`)
7	
8	```json
9	{"origin": "injected|staged|real", "layer": "boundary|filesystem|sandbox|transport|harness|model|gym",
10	 "code": "ENOENT|EACCES|ETIMEDOUT|EINVAL|ESANDBOX|ETRANSPORT|EHARNESS|EMODEL|EGYM|EINTERNAL|ENOEPISODE",
11	 "kind": "missing_file|denied_write|ack_lost|null", "label": "<fixed string below>",
12	 "outcome_known": true|false, "side_effect_applied": true|false|null, "detail": "…"}
13	```
14	
15	| origin / layer | code(s) | label (render verbatim) | outcome_known | side effect |
16	|---|---|---|---|---|
17	| injected / boundary | ENOENT | `simulated: missing file (file still on disk)` | true | no |
18	| injected / boundary | EACCES | `simulated: write denied (nothing written)` | true | no |
19	| injected / boundary | ETIMEDOUT | `simulated: lost ack (write landed; response withheld)` | **false** | **yes** |
20	| staged / filesystem | ENOENT | `staged: file absent since reset` | true | n/a |
21	| real / filesystem | ENOENT, EACCES, EINVAL | `real: OS error in sandbox` | true | no |
22	| real / sandbox | ESANDBOX | `real: sandbox terminated or unavailable` | true | no |
23	| real / transport | ETRANSPORT, ETIMEDOUT | `real: transport failure harness<->sandbox-env (outcome unknown)` | **false** | unknown |
24	| real / harness | EHARNESS | `real: harness worker interrupted mid-call (outcome unknown)` | **false** | unknown (ledger has truth after the fact) |
25	| real / model | EMODEL | `real: model API error` | true | — |
26	| real / gym | EGYM | `real: gym control-plane failure` | true | — |
27	| real / boundary | EINTERNAL, ENOEPISODE | `real: internal error in sandbox-env` | true | no |
28	
29	UI guidance: badge colour by `origin` (injected = amber "simulated", staged = amber "staged", real
30	= red "real"); show `label`; when `outcome_known: false` add "verify before retrying"; when the
31	ledger later resolves `side_effect_applied`, show it ("the write had landed").
32	
33	## Events
34	
35	- `fault.fired` — `FaultFired` + `origin` (`injected` or `staged`). Never `real`.
36	- `interruption` — `Interruption` (always real): `{step, layer, code, label, tool_use_id?, tool?, path?, outcome_known, planned, resumed, worker_generation, at, detail}`. `planned: true` means a scenario `harness_faults` entry triggered it (a deliberate real failure).
37	- `run.resumed` — `{worker_generation, resumed_from_event_id, dangling_tool_use_id?, resumed_at}` emitted by the worker that picked the run up.
38	- `tool.result` — `data.error_class` present iff `is_error`; `data.fault` (FaultFired) present iff a fault fired for this call.
39	
40	## Run status
41	
42	| status | meaning | score |
43	|---|---|---|
44	| `ok` | the agent submitted or ended; evaluation succeeded | number |
45	| `truncated` | step budget exhausted; still evaluated | number |
46	| `unevaluated` | the loop finished but `evaluate` failed (`error_class.layer: gym` or `sandbox`) — **not** the agent's fault | null |
47	| `interrupted` | a real interruption ended the run and no worker resumed it | null |
48	| `error` | the harness could not run the episode (model auth, reset failed, bug) | null |
49	
50	`RunRecord.interruptions[]` lists every real interruption, `worker_generation` is 1 + the number of
51	resumes; a resumed run can still end `ok` (that is the point of the `worker-crash` scenario).
52	
53	## Scenario metadata
54	
55	`Scenario.fault_kinds` (injected kinds in play) and `Scenario.harness_faults[]` (planned **real**
56	interruptions: `{kind: worker_crash|transport_abort, tool, path, nth, after_ms}`) are public so the
57	UI can show "includes a real harness interruption" on the card. The agent is never told either.
58	
59	## Ledger (released at evaluate)
60	
61	`LedgerEntry.origin` (`injected|staged|real`, only when `outcome != ok`), `error_code`, and
62	`interrupted: true` when the harness reported that it never received the response (`POST
63	/episodes/{id}/interruptions`). `verified_before_rewrite` treats `ack_lost` rows and `interrupted`
64	rows identically.
65
